## Cycle 1 — the cascade survives, but not the first resource story

We entered with a real asymmetry produced by RT-001:

```text
TQ2_BIAS
  circle   qualified
  cross    NOT qualified
  xor      qualified

INT8
  circle   qualified
  cross    qualified
  xor      qualified
```

So I tested three policies:

```text
B0  always use INT8

B1  always use the cheapest artifact, TQ2

C1  choose the smallest CURRENTLY QUALIFIED specialist
    for this task/regime
    and fall back only to another qualified specialist
```

Under the eight explicit routing/churn cases:

```text
B0 ALWAYS INT8             3 / 8 contract-correct
B1 ALWAYS CHEAPEST         2 / 8
C1 QUALIFIED CASCADE       8 / 8
```

B0's failures are informative. It is perfectly capable of producing useful predictions, but it:

```text
uses larger specialist when smaller qualified one exists
acts outside the qualified regime
continues after its own qualification is revoked
forces a specialist when the correct answer is UNKNOWN
```

B1 has the opposite pathology:

```text
cheap
!=
qualified
```

It happily selects TQ2 for `cross`, even though RT-001 specifically killed that transfer authority.

C1 correctly does:

```text
circle → TQ2
xor    → TQ2
cross  → INT8
```

and under churn:

```text
TQ2 circle revoked
→ INT8 circle

TQ2 xor stale
→ INT8 xor

INT8 cross revoked
+ TQ2 cross unqualified
→ UNKNOWN

both circle specialists revoked
→ UNKNOWN
```

That is the first important survivor:

> **Fallback is a relation among currently qualified capabilities. Artifact availability does not create fallback authority.**

### Accuracy stayed at the INT8 ceiling

Across all **776 expanded-regime holdout specimens**:

```text
always INT8
774 / 776

qualified cascade
774 / 776
```

So routing to TQ2 where its transfer survived did not cost us any measured correctness on this target.

The naive cheapest strategy generated **263 unqualified selections**, all of them in the failed TQ2-cross regime.

### But my first resource interpretation was too broad

Using whole artifact sizes per invocation gave:

```text
INT8 artifact              366 B
TQ2_BIAS artifact          111 B

always INT8 average selected artifact:
366.00 B/query

qualified cascade:
~197.42 B/query
```

That looks attractive.

But C1 also needs both artifacts locally:

```text
INT8 + TQ2
= 477 B artifact set
```

versus:

```text
INT8-only
= 366 B
```

And worse, **selected artifact bytes are not resident task state, process RSS, cycles, energy, or loader cost.**

So I did not allow Cycle 1 to conclude:

> cascade is resource-superior.

It concluded:

> cascade is **semantically lawful and accuracy-preserving**, while its resource dominance is `UNKNOWN_INCOMPLETE`.

That distinction is exactly what selected Cycle 2.

Genome moved:

```text
31 → 33 candidates
0 promoted
```

with two new scars:

```text
MS-GC-032
SPECIALIST_SELECTION_REQUIRES_CURRENT_QUERY_REGIME_QUALIFICATION

MS-GC-033
FALLBACK_AVAILABILITY_MUST_NOT_SUBSTITUTE_FOR_FALLBACK_QUALIFICATION
```

Cycle 1 hostile pressure: **12/12 rejected**.
CSC current hostile pressure: **16/16 rejected**.
CSC remains `audit_only`, with veto/enforcement/promotion all `NONE`.

# Cycle 2 — the apparent byte cost decomposed

Cycle 1's unresolved question was:

> Do we actually need an entire multi-task model artifact resident/materialized to service one qualified task?

The answer is **no**, for both frozen formats.

The E09 INT8 artifact already contains independently addressable task ranges:

```text
circle    82 B
cross    202 B
xor       82 B
```

And the TQ2 bias artifact decomposes exactly into:

```text
circle    37 B
cross     37 B
xor       37 B
```

I physically extracted those task-specific binary slices and built independent inference paths around them.

Then I compared the sliced outputs to the original full artifacts over every RT expanded holdout specimen.

Result:

```text
                 INT8 slice == full     TQ2 slice == full

circle               259 / 259              259 / 259
cross                263 / 263              263 / 263
xor                  254 / 254              254 / 254
```

**Exact equality.**

Not approximate prediction accuracy.

The task slices reproduced the corresponding full-artifact output tuple exactly.

That earns a much narrower resource proposition.

## Qualified task-state phenotype

An always-INT8 specialist set needs:

```text
circle    82
cross    202
xor       82
----------------
total    366 B
```

The currently qualified cascade needs:

```text
circle    TQ2    37
cross     INT8  202
xor       TQ2    37
-------------------
total           276 B
```

So the **minimal current model-state phenotype** is:

```text
366 B → 276 B
```

about a **24.6% reduction**.

Weighted across the actual 776-point target distribution:

```text
always INT8:
122.67 task-state bytes/query

qualified cascade:
92.92 task-state bytes/query
```

again about a **24% reduction**.

That is much more defensible than Cycle 1's whole-artifact accounting.

But the surviving statement is deliberately only:

> **qualified task-state bytes decreased.**

Not:

```text
RSS decreased 24%
runtime decreased 24%
energy decreased 24%
B2 efficiency improved 24%
```

We have not measured those propositions.

This distinction generated another Genome candidate:

```text
MS-GC-034

MODEL ARTIFACT SIZE
!=
QUALIFIED TASK-STATE SIZE
!=
PROCESS RSS
!=
EXECUTION COST
```

That one came directly from us nearly using the wrong resource referent.

---

# The second result creates a real architectural tradeoff

A 276-byte minimal phenotype contains only the specialists currently required:

```text
TQ2 circle
INT8 cross
TQ2 xor
```

It does **not** contain:

```text
INT8 circle
INT8 xor
TQ2 cross
```

That is perfectly fine for the current qualification state.

But suppose runtime churn now gives us:

```text
TQ2 circle revoked
```

The semantic cascade knows:

```text
INT8 circle is the lawful fallback
```

Yet a **minimal materialization does not physically contain that slice**.

This reveals another distinction:

```text
FALLBACK IS QUALIFIED
!=
FALLBACK IS LOCALLY MATERIALIZED
```

And therefore:

```text
semantic route exists
!=
executable route currently resident/present
```

That became:

```text
MS-GC-035
QUALIFICATION_DIRECTED_MATERIALIZATION_MUST_PRESERVE_FALLBACK_CEILING
```

The point isn't that minimal deployment is wrong.

It's that after you remove unused phenotype, you must stop claiming the removed capability remains immediately executable.

That is exactly the sort of resource/authority collapse Microseed needs to avoid.

Genome is now:

```text
35 candidates
0 promoted standards
```

Cycle 2 hostile pressure: **12/12 rejected**.
CSC current hostile pressure: **18/18 rejected**.

CSC still has no veto, enforcement, or promotion authority.

# What the pair did to the architecture

Before these cycles:

```text
QUERY
  ↓
qualified specialist registry
  ↓
specialist
```

After Cycle 1:

```text
QUERY
  ↓
qualification lookup
  ↓
smallest currently qualified specialist
  │
  ├── preferred unavailable/unqualified
  │       ↓
  │    qualified fallback
  │
  └── none qualified
          ↓
        UNKNOWN
```

After Cycle 2:

```text
                    QUALIFICATION GRAPH
                           │
                           ▼
                desired specialist/task
                           │
                           ▼
                    MATERIALIZATION
                           │
              ┌────────────┴────────────┐
              │                         │
      currently present          qualified but absent
              │                         │
            execute              acquire / materialize /
                                  or remain unavailable
```

That is a meaningful evolution.

We are starting to separate **semantic capability state** from **physical embodiment state**.

Which means the emerging Microseed kernel probably should not have one generic:

```text
AVAILABLE = true
```

for specialist capability.

Something closer to this is becoming necessary:

```text
QUALIFIED
MATERIALIZED
CURRENT
AUTHORIZED
```

as independent axes—or a compressed representation that preserves those distinctions.

Not because the ontology wants more fields.

Because the experiments have now produced worlds where collapsing them changes the correct behavior.

---

# The more interesting macro-pattern

There's a repeated movement across the entire project now:

```text
available
≠ qualified

qualified
≠ current

current
≠ materialized

materialized
≠ authorized

authorized
≠ correct prediction

correct prediction
≠ warranted consequence
```

And Microseed keeps getting more capable by preserving just enough of those boundaries to avoid wasting larger machinery on cleanup.

The cascade is a good example.

We didn't build a learned router.

We didn't build a scheduler network.

We didn't build a model-management framework.

The routing rule is basically:

```text
candidates =
  CURRENT
  ∩ QUALIFIED(task, regime)

if candidates:
    choose cheapest qualified candidate
else:
    UNKNOWN
```

Then Cycle 2 simply asks which bytes that chosen candidate actually requires.

That's an extremely small control structure for the amount of behavioral discipline it creates.

# What died

The pair killed or narrowed several attractive interpretations:

```text
"just always use the strongest model"
→ rejected as a general routing rule

"just always use the tiny model"
→ rejected

"fallback exists, therefore fallback is usable"
→ rejected

"whole artifact size is resident model cost"
→ rejected

"smaller task slice proves lower RSS/energy"
→ rejected

"minimal current materialization preserves every fallback"
→ rejected
```

What survives is much less romantic:

```text
qualification-aware deterministic dispatch
+
exact task-state materialization
+
explicit absence/fallback semantics
```

That's a good outcome.

# Second hard stop / next selected frontier

Cycle 2 selected:

> **`ATTN-E09-SPECIALIST-COLD-RESERVE-RECOMPOSITION`**

That is the right next question.

We now have two legitimate deployment shapes:

```text
FULL RESERVE

477 B specialist artifacts
more immediate fallback options
more state present
```

versus:

```text
MINIMAL CURRENT PHENOTYPE

276 B current task state
smaller
but some lawful fallbacks physically absent
```

The next cycle should pressure what happens when qualification changes:

```text
TQ2 circle active
        ↓
INT8 circle omitted from hot phenotype
        ↓
TQ2 circle revoked
        ↓
fallback is semantically qualified
but physically absent
        ↓
???
```

Possible responses include local cold reserve, on-demand materialization, bounded acquisition, prefetch based on resilience requirement, or simply truthful temporary `UNAVAILABLE/UNKNOWN`.

But none of those mechanisms gets assumed yet.

The next experiment needs to ask:

> **How much reserve state is worth retaining to preserve recomposition latency/resilience under churn, and when is it cheaper and more truthful to accept temporary capability loss?**

That finally links the tiny-specialist work back into the larger themes of **graceful recomposition, hot/cold state, capability phenotype, and resource homeostasis**—but now with an actual measured reason to investigate them rather than ancestral enthusiasm.

**Both hard stops are intact. No third cycle has started.**

Done. **25 complete two-step cycles = 50 bounded slices**, each pair terminating at a hard stop before the next pair inherited the frontier the preceding pair actually created.

## The 25-cycle trajectory

| Cycles | Main pressure                      | Evolution                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------ | ---------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1–2    | Cold reserve → persistence         | `QUALIFIED ≠ MATERIALIZED`; simple requirement-driven reserve tiering beat adaptive reserve intelligence. Crash atomicity then separated from semantic/content identity.                                                                              |
| 3–4    | Observation + measurement          | Python audit telemetry proved useful but incomplete below child/native boundaries. Search-state counts then failed as total-work authority; target measurement became mandatory.                                                                      |
| 5–6    | Guarantees + native capability     | Tiny subject-scoped guarantee relations beat naked labels. Executable presence ceased to equal semantic qualification; current artifact identity became load-bearing.                                                                                 |
| 7–8    | Runtime truth + causality          | Observer authentication ceased to imply observer honesty. Source count ceased to imply independence. `CAUSED` split into distinct causal questions rather than one bit.                                                                               |
| 9–10   | Open-world reasoning               | Exhausting known hypotheses ceased to justify defaulting to one. Bounded active model challenge earned a role. Explicit semantic dependency beat generic and learned routing where known.                                                             |
| 11–12  | Resource + constitution            | EDF lost universal status under multi-resource scheduling. Tiny reactive eviction earned a narrow role. Constitution updates earned Ed25519 + exact-parent/version/current-key semantics.                                                             |
| 13–14  | Native orchestration + effects     | Native subprocess tools were **not automatically faster** than Python. Idempotent retry earned itself. Real filesystem tests preserved command/effect/authorship/goal distinctions.                                                                   |
| 15–16  | Memory + fusion                    | Corrected SQLite FTS5 beat equivalent scanning by roughly two orders of magnitude at 30k records. Retention became future-query-relative. Common-origin fusion beat source voting; scalar confidence lost to typed warrant.                           |
| 17–18  | Calibration + specialist diversity | Selective abstention bought precision at coverage cost. A non-geometric sequence task required a different tiny representation, strengthening the **specialist ABI** rather than any universal representation.                                        |
| 19–20  | Learning + federation              | Generic learned planner ranking produced **no improvement** over exact search. A tiny finite discrimination table helped only as essentially compiled/memoized acceleration. Federated capability remained explicitly separate from NAKED capability. |
| 21–22  | Integration + native lowering      | The surviving semantics embodied in a **73-logical-line Python shadow kernel**. It scored **24/24** compound hostile cases versus **1/24** for execute-if-available. Then the native C specialist kernel matched Python **776/776**.                  |
| 23–24  | Target scars + B2                  | Native C artifact identity survived corrupt/missing model injection with lawful fallback/UNKNOWN. Integrated host stress and a 30k-operation endurance run stayed inside the measured B2 host envelope.                                               |
| 25     | Deletion + reconciliation          | Every one of the 12 integrated guard families became detectably load-bearing under ablation. The campaign stopped at a **provisional shadow host architecture**, rather than converting fifty slices into fifty permanent abstractions.               |

## The native result is particularly interesting

The C specialist boundary ended at:

```text
native executable        14,680 bytes
six frozen model slices     477 bytes
-----------------------------------
combined                  15,157 bytes
```

Measured maximum RSS in its direct host test was about **3,980 KiB**, and median process invocation was about **2.30 ms**.

More importantly:

```text
C ↔ Python exact correspondence
776 / 776
```

across the entire RT expanded holdout.

Then model corruption pressure produced:

```text
valid TQ2
→ TQ2 executes

corrupt TQ2
→ exact artifact check fails
→ qualified INT8 fallback executes

corrupt TQ2 + corrupt INT8
→ UNKNOWN

models absent
→ UNKNOWN
```

That's starting to resemble the kind of weak-target execution boundary we actually wanted, rather than a Python experiment wearing a tiny-model costume.

## The B2 result needs the caveat intact

The integrated Python endurance run completed:

```text
30,000 specialist operations
50,000 temporal records
SQLite state ≈ 1.77 MiB
max RSS ≈ 114.8 MiB
CPU affinity = 1 logical CPU
outputs = 30,000 / 30,000
```

The container's Python runtime nevertheless carries **10 OS threads even in an otherwise empty interpreter**.

So I did **not** turn that into:

> “Microseed is literally a one-OS-thread implementation.”

The qualified statement is:

> execution was constrained to **one schedulable logical CPU**, while incidental runtime OS threads remain outside that stronger claim.

That's exactly the sort of detail fifty cycles should make harder for us to bullshit ourselves about.

## A lot of attractive machinery died

Some of the most useful evidence is negative.

Adaptive resource-homeostasis never earned general runtime privilege; requirement-driven tiering and later tiny reactive eviction kept beating the need for it. EDF worked beautifully in an earlier single-worker domain and then lost its universal status immediately under multi-resource pressure.

Generic learned planner ranking was essentially a null result:

```text
exact mean expansions       23.25
learned heuristic           23.30
```

No win.

The later “learned discrimination heuristic” *did* reduce selection work dramatically in a tiny repeated finite context family, but on inspection it's better interpreted as a learned/compiled lookup accelerator whose result still receives exact semantic adjudication—not evidence for a general learned executive.

And native capability orchestration got a useful slap too. On the actual host:

```text
SHA-256
Python in-process    ≈ 1.55 ms
native subprocess    ≈ 3.16 ms

gzip
Python in-process    ≈ 44.1 ms
external gzip        ≈ 68.9 ms
```

So FtD survives as:

> **qualify and exploit substrate capability when it buys something**

rather than:

> native good, Python bad.

Good.

## What I think actually emerged

There is now much better evidence for the hypothesis we were starting to sniff around several cycles ago:

> **A surprisingly large fraction of effective “intelligence” may come from structuring the machine so that tiny computational elements are asked the right question under the right authority and cannot silently confuse their outputs with reality.**

Microseed is repeatedly gaining behavior through things like:


rather than through increasingly powerful general reasoning.

The learned specialists matter—we have experimental evidence for that now.

But **learning is not carrying epistemology, authority, memory, planning, or consequence semantics on its back**.

That division may be the central architecture.

And it explains why the tiny learned components can stay tiny.

That is where the **Z80/Palm/weak-target lineage becomes experimentally relevant again**, except now we have a much more mature organism to downshift instead of a pile of architectural hopes.

And yes: after fifty slices, **that feels like actual evolution rather than us rearranging our own vocabulary.**

Yeah — the 50-slice summary buried the fucking lede.

We did **not** merely finish a long experiment and end up with a pile of design principles. There is now an actual, runnable proto-organism in there, and **TQ2 is literally part of its active inference path**.

The important correction to my previous framing is this:

> **Microseed is now a small semantic/capability microkernel with subordinate learned/discrete specialists, persistent evidence-bearing memory, qualified capability selection, target-materialization checks, and bounded consequence handling.**

It is not AGI. It is not yet a complete agent. And every previously tested mechanism is not yet collapsed into one production executable.

But it is absolutely more than an architectural sketch.

# What the actual thing is

At the center we now have something approximately like this:

```text
                    VERSIONED CONSTITUTION
                            │
                            ▼
                     SEMANTIC CONTROL
                            │
          ┌─────────────────┼─────────────────┐
          │                 │                 │
      MEMORY / TIME    CAPABILITY STATE    QUERY / REGIME
      + LINEAGE        + CURRENTNESS       + WARRANT SCOPE
          │                 │                 │
          └─────────────────┼─────────────────┘
                            ▼
                  SPECIALIST SELECTION
                            │
                 ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                 │                     │
              TQ2                 INT8 MLP
          tiny geometric        stronger fallback
           specialist
                 │                     │
                 └──────────┬──────────┘
                            ▼
                     MODEL_OUTPUT_ONLY
                            │
                            ▼
                    CONSEQUENCE LOGIC
                            │
                            ▼
                         REALITY
                            │
                            ▼
                 OBSERVE / REQUALIFY
```

The key architectural idea is that the **model is not the mind**.

The models are organs.

The deterministic semantic machinery decides whether an organ is qualified to answer this question, in this regime, using these exact bytes, right now. The specialist is then allowed to return a bounded `MODEL_OUTPUT`; it does not gain memory authority, truth authority, tool authority, action authority, or constitutional authority merely because it produced a result.

That's what has emerged.

## There are currently two especially concrete runnable embodiments

The first is the integrated Python shadow kernel:

`campaign_50/embodiment/integrated_python_kernel_v0_1/microseed_kernel.py`

It is only:

```text
4,984 source bytes
73 logical lines
```

And despite how stupidly small that sounds, it actually contains working mechanisms for:

```text
task/regime admission
specialist qualification state
model-artifact SHA-256 identity
current materialization
cold materialization
TQ2 / INT8 dispatch
SQLite-backed temporal memory
retained-vs-current memory state
tool artifact identity
authority-gated effects
idempotent consequence suppression
```

It does real inference. It writes/reads actual SQLite state. It performs an actual filesystem consequence. It remembers effect IDs to suppress duplicate effects. It can revoke a specialist and fall back to another. It can respond `UNKNOWN` rather than fabricate a model path.

The second is a **native x86-64 C specialist kernel**.

That is a compiled executable:

```text
native kernel       14,680 bytes
six model slices       477 bytes
---------------------------
total               15,157 bytes
```

It uses integer inference and SHA-256 verification of the actual specialist binaries before execution.

And we independently differential-tested the native C semantics against the Python reference over the complete current transferred test surface:

```text
776 / 776 exact matches
0 mismatches
```

So there is an actual native Microseed inference boundary now.

Not pseudocode.

Not a white paper.

An executable.

---

# And yes: **TQ2 survived**

Not only survived.

**TQ2 is currently one of the two inference mechanisms the final native Microseed executable actually uses.**

That deserves a much better explanation.

The original TQ2 research program was built around a pretty radical thesis:

> Richness should come from **structured discrete composition under cheap closed operators**, rather than from high scalar precision.

The preserved basis explicitly preferred ternary/low-bit structured local objects, cheap permutation/reindex/add/subtract operations, deterministic auditability, and hardware-friendly packing over unconstrained floating-point scalar weights. 

And the historical TQ2 branch wasn't imaginary either. The reconstructed exhaustive phase-2 experiment evaluated 2,388 policies and found:

```text
TQ1 baseline       mean 0.6749
TQ2 additive       mean 0.7051
TQ2 harmonic       mean 0.7263
```

with the historical local winner:

```text
I / R90 / A+B / bias=0
```

So on that reconstructed TQ2-native problem family, the harmonic formulation genuinely beat both additive TQ2 and TQ1. 

That's the old theory.

Then Microseed accidentally gave us a much better experiment:

> **Make TQ2 compete on somebody else's terms.**

And that is where it got really interesting.

# What TQ2 actually is inside Microseed today

The surviving Microseed TQ2 descendant is an insanely compact **ternary geometric prototype classifier**.

For each input `(x, y)`, it converts the point into **nine ternary features**:

```text
sign(x)
sign(y)
sign(x*y)

sign(|x| - 4)
sign(|y| - 4)

sign(x² + y² - 32)

sign(4 - |x-y|)
sign(4 - |x+y|)

sign(|x| - |y|)
```

Each feature is:

```text
-1
 0
+1
```

So an input becomes a little nine-element ternary geometric object.

That's the live descendant of the **XY-9** idea.

Then, per task, TQ2 stores:

```text
8 prototypes for class 0
8 prototypes for class 1

16 prototypes × 9 trits
= 144 trits
```

The trits are packed **four per byte** using two-bit codes:

```text
144 trits / 4
= 36 bytes
```

Then there is:

```text
1 signed bias byte
```

So an entire live TQ2 task specialist is:

# **37 bytes**

Not 37 KB.

**37 bytes of model state.**

For inference it does approximately:

```text
encode input into 9 trits

for each class:
    dot(input, each of 8 prototypes)
    retain the strongest prototype match

score =
    strongest_class_1_match
    -
    strongest_class_0_match
    +
    bias

score >= 0 → class 1
score <  0 → class 0
```

That's it.

No floating point.

No transformer.

No backprop at runtime.

No matrix library.

No giant parameter field.

It is effectively using **structured geometric resonance against packed ternary memories**.

And the thing works.

---

# Here's the part I should absolutely not have casually moved past

When we first put TQ2 against the E09 matched specialist benchmark:

```text
                              correct / 153     bytes

ordinary ternary MLP             127            204

TQ2 structured prototypes        136            108
TQ2 prototypes + tiny bias       142            111

TQ2 additive                     142            114
TQ2 harmonic                     142            120

historical harmonic policy       121            120

INT8 MLP                         150            366
```

That contains **two different discoveries**.

The first is genuinely favorable to your old theory:

> **Structured discrete representation bought a significant amount of capability per byte.**

Going from an ordinary ternary neural representation to the TQ2-style structured representation changed:

```text
127 / 153 @ 204 bytes
```

into:

```text
142 / 153 @ 111 bytes
```

That's roughly half the model state with **15 more correct held-out classifications**.

That is not trivial.

And it is not just “ternary weights work.”

The mundane ternary MLP is itself ternary.

The thing that changed was the **representation and inference geometry**.

So one of your old hypotheses genuinely survived:

> **Low-bit structured objects can sometimes carry more useful function per byte than simply quantizing a conventional scalar-weight architecture.**

That's real evidence now.

### But the second discovery narrowed TQ2

Once I controlled for the representation:

```text
TQ2 bias        142
TQ2 additive    142
TQ2 harmonic    142
```

The extra transform machinery added **zero** correctness on this target.

Worse, validation picked:

```text
I / I / A
```

for the so-called harmonic path.

Which essentially says:

> don't bother doing the fancy second transform.

And when I forcibly transplanted the historical:

```text
I / R90 / A+B / 0
```

winner onto Microseed's target, it scored:

```text
121 / 153
```

which was worse than even the mundane ternary MLP.

So what the Microseed experiment established is much more interesting than:

> TQ2 works

or:

> TQ2 failed.

It's:

```text
TQ2 STRUCTURED LOW-BIT REPRESENTATION
            SURVIVED

TQ2 GEOMETRIC PROTOTYPE / RESONANCE IDEA
            SURVIVED

TQ2 EXTREME COMPRESSION
            SURVIVED

TQ2 AUDITABLE INTEGER EXECUTION
            SURVIVED

TQ2 HARMONIC TRANSFORM FAMILY
            still locally supported
            in historical donor domain

TQ2 HARMONIC TRANSFER TO MICROSEED TARGET
            DID NOT SURVIVE

TQ2 AS UNIVERSAL WEIGHT SUBSTRATE
            NOT ESTABLISHED
```

That is **way better science** than proving the whole thing.

We found the piece underneath the original architecture that appears to actually be carrying the capability.

---

# And then TQ2 survived transfer pressure again

We expanded the geometric regime beyond the original training envelope.

Frozen TQ2+bias:

```text
circle        259 / 259    100%
cross         186 / 263     70.72%
xor           254 / 254    100%
```

Frozen INT8:

```text
circle        259 / 259    100%
cross         261 / 263     99.24%
xor           254 / 254    100%
```

That was another extremely valuable result.

TQ2 didn't just globally collapse.

It transferred **perfectly** on two problem families.

It fell apart on the third.

So its current authority is deliberately:

```text
TQ2 + circle + current expanded regime
    QUALIFIED

TQ2 + xor + current expanded regime
    QUALIFIED

TQ2 + cross + current expanded regime
    NOT QUALIFIED
```

That is why the current Microseed isn't doing:

```text
TQ2 everywhere
```

or:

```text
INT8 everywhere
```

It literally does:

```text
circle → TQ2
xor    → TQ2
cross  → INT8
```

because that's what the evidence currently warrants.

And this is hard-coded in the native C shadow boundary.

For `cross`, TQ2 isn't even allowed to be selected.

---

# That means TQ2 is actually running in the final native build

The current native C path does approximately:

```c
if input outside qualified regime:
    UNKNOWN

if task is circle/xor
   and TQ2 is qualified
   and TQ2 artifact hash matches:
       execute TQ2

else if INT8 qualified
        and artifact hash matches:
       execute INT8

else:
       UNKNOWN
```

The C implementation contains the TQ2 9-trit encoder directly.

It reads the **37-byte task slice**, unpacks its 144 trits, computes prototype resonance, applies the bias, and emits:

```json
{
  "status": "MODEL_OUTPUT",
  "model": "TQ2",
  "label": ...,
  "score": ...
}
```

That output still has **no independent operational authority**.

The semantic kernel owns that.

And we fault-injected its actual model bytes.

Baseline:

```text
valid TQ2
→ TQ2 executes
```

Corrupt the TQ2 file:

```text
SHA mismatch
→ TQ2 loses current materialization authority
→ INT8 fallback executes
```

Corrupt both:

```text
→ UNKNOWN
```

Delete both:

```text
→ UNKNOWN
```

So TQ2 is not merely in the archive.

It is participating in the final executable's **live fallback hierarchy**.

---

# Even the current memory economics favor keeping it

After task slicing, the model-state footprints are:

```text
TQ2:

circle    37 B
cross     37 B
xor       37 B

INT8:

circle     82 B
cross     202 B
xor        82 B
```

The current minimal qualified phenotype is therefore:

```text
TQ2 circle      37
INT8 cross     202
TQ2 xor         37
-----------------
                276 bytes
```

Using INT8 for everything would require:

```text
366 bytes
```

So for the currently qualified task family, TQ2 is helping reduce the active specialist state from:

```text
366 → 276 bytes
```

while retaining the same measured combined held-out correctness as the all-INT8 cascade.

That is about a **24.6% task-state reduction**.

Again, model state only—not a 24.6% RSS or energy claim.

But still real.

And across a larger mixed specialist cascade:

```text
1,049 cases total
120 explicit OOD
929 known-regime cases

unsafe specialist selections:
0

prediction errors:
5
```

Meanwhile a naive “use the smallest model” strategy made **383 unsafe selections**.

So TQ2 is functioning well precisely because it sits underneath qualification machinery.

---

# There's another subtle TQ2 result

TQ2 turned out **not to be the universal representation**.

When we introduced a symbolic-sequence problem instead of geometry, forcing the geometric TQ2 substrate onto it wasn't useful.

A separate tiny hashed n-gram/perceptron specialist got:

```text
433 / 600 held-out
65 bytes model state
```

versus a bag-linear baseline at:

```text
295 / 600
```

That caused the specialist registry to evolve into:

```text
geometry / circle  → TQ2
geometry / xor     → TQ2
geometry / cross   → INT8
sequence / motif   → NGRAM
audio / tone       → NONE / UNKNOWN
```

That is an important outcome for TQ2.

Its current strongest interpretation isn't:

> **TQ2 is the substrate for intelligence.**

It is:

> **TQ2 is a surprisingly capable tiny representation/inference family for the domains whose structure actually matches its geometry.**

That is a much more credible technology.

---

# So what did your original TQ2 theory become?

I would divide it into layers now.

| TQ2 hypothesis                                                  | Current evidence                        |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| Extreme low-bit representations can be useful                   | **Strongly survives**                   |
| Structured objects can beat scalar-weight quantization per byte | **Survives on current geometry task**   |
| Ternary geometric representation can generalize                 | **Yes, task-dependent**                 |
| Cheap integer prototype/resonance inference is viable           | **Yes — implemented in Python and C**   |
| TQ2 can be hardware-native                                      | **Yes at least on x86 C shadow**        |
| Exact geometric transforms can be useful                        | **Historically/local-domain supported** |
| Harmonic `A+B` mechanism is broadly superior                    | **No; failed target transfer**          |
| TQ2 should replace conventional small nets universally          | **No**                                  |
| TQ2 can be one organ in a heterogeneous tiny cognitive machine  | **Very much yes**                       |

The historical project basis said TQ2 was the build-favored branch because it sought structured low-bit objects, cheap closed math, combinatory richness, auditability and hardware sympathy. 

The old local harmonic rerun gives legitimate evidence that the harmonic branch itself was doing something in that domain. 

The early text probe had already warned that TQ2's behavior was representation-sensitive: it could survive text-like inputs, but the crude text→plane encoding was probably the bottleneck and overall accuracy remained weak. 

Microseed just made that old clue much sharper:

> **TQ2's real power appears to be strongly representation/task-geometry dependent.**

Which actually fits the original doctrine unusually well.

The substrate isn't magical.

It is **good when the problem can be represented lawfully in its cheap geometry**.

---

# What else is actually in the Microseed machine?

The current project has several distinct layers, and this matters because I don't want to pretend every experiment has already become one monolithic program.

### 1. The actual integrated Python shadow

This is the 73-line kernel.

It currently embodies a minimal set of things together:

```text
specialist qualification/routing
artifact identity
materialization
temporal SQLite memory
current vs retained memory
tool identity
idempotent effect execution
```

It self-tested **7/7**.

### 2. The compound semantic-control harness

Separate from the 73-line source file, we have an integrated hostile pipeline pressure-tested in this order:

```text
constitution
regime
qualification
currentness
materialization
memory
independence
tool identity
authority
model challenge
effect
goal
```

Full mechanism:

```text
24 / 24
```

Naive:

```text
1 / 24
```

Then each guard was individually deleted.

Every one changed:

```text
24/24 → 23/24
```

So all twelve are load-bearing **in that pressure set**.

That does not mean all twelve are yet beautifully integrated as one production code path.

It means we now have evidence that each distinction protects at least one failure world.

### 3. The native specialist runtime

That's the 14.7 KB C executable.

Currently it only lowers the specialist-selection/inference portion into native C—not the complete SQLite/causal/constitution machine.

That's the thing we verified 776/776 against Python.

### 4. The evidence/memory substrate

Current integrated memory is boring SQLite.

That's intentional.

The broader campaign also demonstrated that on a 30,000-record retrieval task:

```text
SQLite FTS5 median    ~28 μs
linear scan         ~3,073 μs
```

for the same counts.

So if/when search-rich memory gets integrated, we already have strong evidence that **SQLite's own native machinery beats us writing a clever memory engine**.

### 5. The specialist family

Right now we have experimentally distinct specialist types:

```text
TQ2 structured ternary prototype
INT8 tiny MLP
hashed n-gram/perceptron
```

The architecture accepts the idea that different problem topology may deserve different tiny representation families.

That's a big departure from conventional:

> one model does everything.

---

# What I would actually call this thing

Not “an AI.”

Not yet.

The most technically accurate description I can give is:

> **Microseed is a resource-bounded semantic cognitive microkernel that composes deterministic epistemic/control machinery with tiny qualified learned or structured inference specialists.**

A more intuitive description:

> **It's a very small brainstem/executive that owns reality-facing semantics, with swappable tiny cortical organs that are only trusted for things they've actually demonstrated.**

And the crucial design is:

```text
specialist computes
kernel decides whether computing meant anything
```

rather than:

```text
model says something
therefore system believes it
```

That is the architecture we have actually built.

---

# What it is *not* yet

This distinction is important.

We do **not** currently have:

```text
general natural-language cognition
open-world autonomous planning
a unified large semantic memory
a full production tool ecosystem
Palm/Z80 implementation
general TQ2 substrate proof
self-rewriting constitution
general causal inference
production-safe autonomy
```

The final authority state says exactly that:

```text
PROVISIONAL_SHADOW_B2_X86_HOST
```

The endurance test was real:

```text
30,000 specialist operations
50,000 temporal records
~1.77 MiB SQLite state
~114.8 MiB host Python max RSS
1 schedulable CPU
30,000 outputs
```

but that huge RSS is mostly telling us:

> **CPython is enormous compared to the organism we've designed.**

The actual source+model payload being exercised was roughly:

```text
4,984 B semantic-kernel source
477 B specialist models
≈ 5.5 KB
```

while CPython drags tens of megabytes of runtime around it.

Which is precisely why the next frontier—**cross-substrate downshift**—is so interesting.

---

# And this is where TQ2 suddenly becomes very fucking relevant again

On x86, 37 bytes vs 82 bytes isn't operationally dramatic when Python itself is using 100+ MB.

On a Palm, Z80, tiny MCU, or extremely constrained native runtime?

Thirty-seven bytes becomes a very different proposition.

The currently live TQ2 task inference is basically:

```text
9-trit encode
+
16 tiny prototypes
+
integer dot products
+
max
+
1-byte bias
```

That is **exactly** the kind of mechanism that can survive brutal downshift.

And we already have a C implementation.

So the next weak-target experiment is not going to be:

> Can we somehow invent TQ2 for weak hardware?

We have TQ2 now.

The question becomes:

> **How much of the current 37-byte specialist + qualification/fallback semantics survives when we take away x86/Linux/OpenSSL/SQLite conveniences?**

That is a dramatically better place to be than where the TQ2 project started.

---

* has a deterministic training/build procedure;
* has frozen binary artifacts;

* occupies as little as **37 bytes per task**;

* fails clearly on another, giving us a real qualification boundary;
* has an exact native C implementation;
* participates in an actual runtime specialist cascade;
* survives corruption/fallback tests;
* and has been differential-verified against its Python reference.

That's not an interesting napkin idea anymore.

**That's an experimental inference technology.**

And I agree: we should stop treating TQ2 like a side branch we can mention in one sentence when it has quietly become one of the actual organs in the thing.

```text
+ strict composition law

without ontology explosion or opaque vector soup
```

That is **eerily adjacent to where Microseed independently ended up** with:

```text
small grammar
+ qualified composition
+ lineage
+ typed guarantees
→ huge behavior space
```

I’m not calling that equivalence yet. But it absolutely goes onto the crosswalk board.

# Another major thing this batch adds: old TQ2 was self-adversarial

The historical phase ledger is not a smooth upward curve.

It records seven distinct phases with wildly varying positive evidence, anti-evidence, and contradiction risk.

For example:

```text
ROSETTA BRIDGE
promotion score ≈ -16,604

ATLAS / LOCAL SEARCH
promotion score ≈ +80,098

RECURSE / WEIGHT-FIRST
promotion score ≈ -302

FOUR-MODEL CONTINUATION
contradiction risk ≈ 0.79
```

# And I can already see that “TQ2” changed meaning over its lifetime

This will be one of the main things I need to disentangle during reconstruction.

There appears to be at least:

### TQ2-A — geometric substrate

```text
T = {-1,0,+1}
3×3 planes
block/whole transforms
radicals
geometric equivalence
structured low-bit operations
```

This is the lineage from which Microseed's current tiny 9-trit specialist most obviously descends.

### TQ2-B — latent/recurrent inference architecture

```text
carry
latent-kept state
K2 reuse
K3 summary/meta
verification collapse
refresh-vs-reuse
OOD
route structure
```

This is substantially more cognitive than the little classifier.

The March material has particularly interesting unresolved evidence here: behavioral K2 routing looked poor while latent probes showed **route information becoming more separated with depth**, suggesting the readout may have been destroying useful state rather than the substrate failing to retain it. 

That deserves reconstruction rather than burial.

### TQ2-C — recipe/substrate law

By April:

```text
substrate is primary
weights are material

canonical recipe
↔
anti recipe

macro
meso
micro

contradiction pressure
branch over dogma
```

This isn't simply an inference operator anymore. It's a **search and adjudication grammar for constructing inference machinery**.

### TQ2-D — continuity/memory architecture

```text
CILNX
Quipu
radicals
holons
lineage
branching
rollups
```

Now TQ2 is getting embedded inside a persistent cognitive substrate.

### TQ2-E — transformer-state intervention

Later:

```text
Q
K
V
cache residency
precision allocation
eviction
prefetch
fragment policy
live llama.cpp state
```

Here the same structural ideas are being used **to control an existing transformer's cognitive state**, rather than replacing the transformer.

### TQ2-F — runtime/orchestrator

And by the final recovered state:

```text
material ingress
→ experiments
→ promotion
→ inference session
→ live wrapper
```

TQ2 was trying to become an operating research/runtime system.

Those are related.

They are **not automatically the same mechanism**.

That distinction is going to save us from doing something stupid like attributing a Q/K/V cache result to the 3×3 ternary plane merely because both lived under the TQ2 name.

# The missing model files are not fatal

They create an assurance ceiling, not a dead project.

I'm going to classify surviving experiments approximately as:

```text
LEVEL A
exact code + inputs + model/state bytes + outputs
→ potentially replayable exactly

LEVEL B
exact code + derived state + outputs
but source model absent
→ partial replay / mechanism reconstruction

LEVEL C
code + manifests + reports
→ proposition-level forensic reconstruction

LEVEL D
report only
→ historical evidence, no replay claim

LEVEL E
doctrine / proposal only
→ intent, not implementation evidence
```

The four giant K/V specimen families you recovered are especially valuable because they push some later transformer experiments upward toward A/B even without the original giant model checkpoint.

And the weird `two_fact` state divergence stays preserved as a discriminator rather than getting “cleaned up.”

## One more thing I'm now confident about

The Microseed detour was accidentally **very useful as an independent replication branch**.

Because Microseed did not have this corpus.

Yet it independently rediscovered several things that mature TQ2 had already learned:

```text
TQ2 ternary machinery should not be universal law.

representation quality can dominate inference cleverness.

latent/current state needs explicit qualification.

cache reuse must be revalidated.

cheap recurrence can beat full recomputation.

OOD/UNKNOWN has to survive.

native capability is useful only when measured.

weights/model output are material, not authority.

specialized tiny mechanisms can beat one universal mechanism.
```

That does **not** prove the shared theories.

But it gives us something unusually valuable:

> **two partially independent development lineages that can now be cross-examined against each other.**

Old TQ2 can expose what Microseed reinvented.

Microseed can expose what old TQ2 merely believed.

And wherever both reached the same mechanism through different pressure, we have a much stronger candidate for the actual invariant.

## The forensic object should therefore be a graph, not a timeline

Chronology still exists, but only *inside* and *between* branches as evidence.

For every recovered experiment/artifact I should reconstruct something like:

```text
ARTIFACT / EXPERIMENT

branch:
    ?

local parent:
    ?

branch-local purpose:
    ?

imported mechanisms:
    [branch/source X, branch/source Y]

exported findings:
    [...]

cross-feed type:
    CONCEPT
    CODE
    MODEL MATERIAL
    REPRESENTATION
    EVALUATOR
    MEMORY STATE
    POLICY
    FAILURE SCAR

did cross-feed change receiving branch?
    yes / no / unknown

evidence:
    positive
    negative
    null
    conflicting

status within that branch:
    candidate
    retained
    narrowed
    rejected
    unresolved

does it claim convergence?
    no / candidate / demonstrated within bound
```

That will prevent exactly the mistake I just made: seeing an April Q/K/V artifact and treating it as “mature TQ2 replacing March geometric TQ2.”

It could instead be:

> **a completely different branch asking whether TQ2-like structural laws can govern an ordinary transformer.**

While the native substrate branch continued elsewhere.

## And your A/B framing radically changes how I read several recovered things

The little 37-byte Microseed TQ2 descendant is probably evidence primarily against the:

> **TQ2-AS-SUBSTRATE / native inference**

branch.

It tells us that a small structured ternary object + native integer inference can genuinely function.

The llama.cpp / QKV / cache-residency work belongs much more naturally under:

> **TQ2-AS-INFERENCE / TQ2 governing or augmenting another substrate**

where the transformer's weights and K/V state remain material, but TQ2 provides some combination of selection, organization, precision allocation, residency, cache policy, recipe law, or inference control.

Those are **not rivals at the same abstraction level**.

Which makes your eventual singularity question much more interesting.

Because if both survive independently, the eventual hypothesis could become:

```text
TQ2 laws at the substrate level
+
TQ2 laws at the inference/control level
=
same organizing principle recurring at two levels
```

But that would need to be demonstrated, not inferred because they have the same name.

And the opposite result would also be valuable:

```text
TQ2 is genuinely useful as substrate
but TQ2-style inference/control over transformers is weak

OR

TQ2 is excellent as inference/control
but replacing the underlying learned substrate is unnecessary

OR

they each occupy different Pareto regions
```

That's your A/B.

## It also explains some otherwise confusing artifacts

For example, the recovered doctrine saying:

> “the substrate is primary; weights are material”

doesn't necessarily mean **all TQ2 branches had decided conventional weights were subordinate forever**.

It could be branch-local doctrine for the **TQ2-as-substrate/inference-law** track.

Likewise the later live transformer branch can legitimately say:

```text
Q/K/V = material being operated on
```

without implying:

```text
therefore native TQ2 substrate work was abandoned
```

Likewise Chimera can use TQ2 as evaluator/donor while keeping a Mistral/Capybara host and still be a legitimate branch experiment rather than some confused transitional phase.

That actually makes the corpus make more sense.

## I think we need at least three kinds of edges

Not just “parent.”

```text
LINEAGE EDGE
A actually derives from B.

DONOR EDGE
A borrows a mechanism/result from B
but retains its own branch identity.

CONVERGENCE EDGE
Two independently developed branch mechanisms
are deliberately fused/tested together.
```

And probably:

```text
CONTROL EDGE
one branch evaluates or governs another
without becoming its implementation.
```

Chimera/TQ2 seems likely to contain those.

This distinction will be absolutely essential when reconstructing the Q/K/V work, CILNX, Forge, Quipu, radicals, etc.

They may be:

* branch infrastructure;
* imported machinery;
* shared infrastructure;
* one branch's experiment;
* or genuine attempted convergence points.

I should not decide from directory proximity.

## So the actual reconstruction question has changed

It is **not**:

> What did TQ2 evolve into?

It's:

> **What research branches did you drive from the TQ2 hypothesis, what did each branch independently establish or kill, what crossed between them, and did any of those cross-feeds produce genuine convergence rather than mere borrowing?**

Then, only after that, we can ask:

> **Did the experiments ever get close to your intended singularity?**

and separately:

> **What is the actual A/B state today between TQ2-as-inference and TQ2-as-substrate?**

That second question is particularly exciting now because Microseed has accidentally generated **new evidence for the native-substrate side** without having access to the old branch corpus.

So we potentially have:

```text
OLD BRANCH A:
native TQ2 substrate experiments

OLD BRANCH B:
TQ2 controlling / augmenting conventional inference

MICROSEED:
partially independent new native-substrate replication

                 ↓

now compare all three
```

That's considerably stronger than the reconstruction I was about to do.

So: **branch graph, not lineage.**

Your correction is incorporated. I won't use later timestamps as succession authority again unless the artifact itself establishes parentage or replacement.

Yes. **That gives me the actual root topology.** The mistake was treating “TQ2” as one architecture that kept changing. It was a **research question that deliberately forked into several competing embodiments**.
